| 966 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, The earth's crust moves slowly. Today, Dr. Mori meets us at the entrance of the earthquake corner. On a table, there are two wooden blocks with a thin sponge between them. Push them together, Dr. Mori says. Haru pushes slowly. At first, the blocks don't move. Then, snap! They slip suddenly, and the table shakes a little. That's how an earthquake works, Dr. Mori explains. Plates push against each other for a long time. Then the rocks under the ground break and move suddenly, and the ground shakes. An earthquake happens when the ground moves. Say it with me. An earthquake happens when the ground moves. Great! Let's read the panel. "Why Do Earthquakes Happen? Deep under the ground, huge plates push against each other. Little by little, the push gets stronger. One day, the rocks break and move suddenly. Then the ground shakes. This is an earthquake. Japan has many earthquakes, so it is important to be ready. During the shaking, protect your head. Stay away from windows and tall shelves, because things might fall. At home, fix tall furniture to the wall." Why does the ground shake? Because rocks under the ground break and move suddenly. What should you fix to the wall? Tall furniture. Ember says, When we are ready, we can feel safe and strong. That's the importance of learning! Now, phonics. Let's make nouns with a, n, c, e. Distant, distance. Keep a safe distance from windows. Enter, entrance. The e in e, r disappears. Important, importance. Guide, guidance. Take away the e. Thank you for your guidance, Dr. Mori! Next time, we will visit the Volcano Watch Center and learn about typhoons and heavy rain! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, The earth's crust moves slowly. Let's read the panel. "Why Do Earthquakes Happen? Deep under the ground, huge plates push against each other. Little by little, the push gets stronger. One day, the rocks break and move suddenly. Then the ground shakes. This is an earthquake. Japan has many earthquakes, so it is important to be ready. During the shaking, protect your head. | 967 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, An earthquake happens when the ground moves. Today, Dr. Mori shows us the Volcano Watch Center. It's very quiet inside. In the silence, we can hear the computers humming. Many screens show Mount Cinder from different cameras. The mountain is calm, as usual. On one big screen, there is a white swirl of clouds far out at sea. Dr. Mori points to it. This is a typhoon. It's very far away, but we watch the weather every day, too. A typhoon brings strong wind and heavy rain. Say it with me. A typhoon brings strong wind and heavy rain. Great! Let's read the weather card next to the screen. "All About Typhoons. Typhoons form over warm ocean water. They come mostly in summer and autumn. A typhoon brings strong wind and heavy rain. The center of a typhoon is called the eye. It is calm there. Heavy rain can cause floods and landslides. During a typhoon, stay inside and keep away from windows. In heavy rain, never go near rivers or the sea." Where do typhoons form? Over warm ocean water. Where should you be during a typhoon? Inside, away from the windows. Ember says, I love water, but I never go near the river in heavy rain! Dr. Mori smiles. When you know what to do, you can face a typhoon with confidence. Now, phonics. Let's change e, n, t to e, n, c, e. Silent, silence. Different, difference. What's the difference between the eye and the rest of the typhoon? The eye is calm. Confident, confidence. Patient, patience. Watching the weather every single day takes patience! Next time, we will walk from Harbor Town up to Hilltop Park, the island's tsunami evacuation place! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, An earthquake happens when the ground moves. | 969 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, If you hear a warning, go to high ground. Today, we are back in Harbor Town. On a tall pole, there is a big loudspeaker. Ember explains. In an emergency, the island uses these speakers to tell everyone what to do. After you hear an announcement, tell your family what it said. For example, The announcement said to stay inside. Say it with me. The announcement said to stay inside. Great! Now, let's practice. Ding, dong, ding, dong. A calm, clear voice comes from the speaker. Let's read the announcement. "Ding, dong. This is a drill. This is Harbor Town. A strong typhoon is coming near the island tonight. Please stay inside your house. Close your windows and curtains, and keep away from the windows. Do not go near rivers or the sea. Please listen to the radio or TV for more information. This is a drill. I'll say it again." What did the announcement say? It said to stay inside. Where should you not go? Near rivers or the sea. Haru repeats the main point. The announcement said to stay inside. Riko adds, It also said to close the windows. Ember gives everyone a thumbs-up. Good listening! If you can't hear an announcement clearly, ask an adult nearby. Ember loves his island, and he checks the speakers with the town workers every month. That's real loyalty! Now, phonics. Let's add t, y to make nouns. Safe, safety. Keep the e! Loyal, loyalty. Honest, honesty. Just add y. If you didn't hear something, say so. That's honesty. Certain, certainty. When we listen well, we know what to do with certainty. Next time, we will have a big Disaster Science Quiz at the museum! | Hello, everyone! | 970 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, The announcement said to stay inside. Today is the Disaster Science Quiz at the museum! Two teams sit behind big red buzzers. Team Lava is Haru and Kiko. Team Magma is Riko and Birimo. Dr. Mori is the quiz master, and Ember keeps the score. Dr. Mori reads the first question. What should you do during a typhoon? Say it with me. What should you do during a typhoon? Great! Beep! Kiko presses the buzzer. You should stay inside and keep away from windows. Correct! Let's read the next quiz cards. "Quiz Card Two. True or false? A tsunami is only one big wave. Quiz Card Three. What do we call melted rock under the ground? Quiz Card Four. Where do typhoons form? Quiz Card Five. During an earthquake, what should you protect first? Quiz Card Six. Why does our island have rich soil? Bonus Question. What number should you call for a fire or an ambulance in Japan?" True or false? A tsunami is only one big wave. False! More than one wave can come. What number should you call for a fire or an ambulance? One, one, nine. The answers keep coming. Magma! Over warm ocean water! Your head! Old ash from the volcano! In the end, it's a tie. Ember cheers, Everybody wins! Suddenly, the Safety Lantern glows, and the second window lights up! Now, phonics. Let's review. Assist, assistance. Thanks for your assistance, Ember! Absent, absence. Everyone came today, so there was no absence. Emergency, with e, n, c, y at the end. Safe, safety. Keep the e. Erupt, eruption. Mount Cinder's last eruption was about 300 years ago. Next time, we will pack an emergency kit together! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, The announcement said to stay inside. Today is the Disaster Science Quiz at the museum! Two teams sit behind big red buzzers. Team Lava is Haru and Kiko. Team Magma is Riko and Birimo. Dr. Mori is the quiz master, and Ember keeps the score. Dr. Mori reads the first question. What should you do during a typhoon? Say it with me. What should you do during a typhoon? Great! Beep! Kiko presses the buzzer. You should stay inside and keep away from windows. Correct! Let's read the next quiz cards. "Quiz Card Two. True or false? A tsunami is only one big wave. Quiz Card Three. What do we call melted rock under the ground? Quiz Card Four. Where do typhoons form? Quiz Card Five. During an earthquake, what should you protect first? Quiz Card Six. Why does our island have rich soil? Bonus Question. What number should you call for a fire or an ambulance in Japan?" True or false? A tsunami is only one big wave. False! More than one wave can come. What number should you call for a fire or an ambulance? One, one, nine. The answers keep coming. Magma! Over warm ocean water! Your head! Old ash from the volcano! Thanks for your assistance, Ember! Absent, absence. Everyone came today, so there were no absences. Emergency, with e, n, c, y at the end. Safe, safety. Keep the e. Erupt, eruption. Mount Cinder's last eruption was about 300 years ago. Next time, we will pack an emergency kit together! | A+B | 'there was no absence' is not natural; countable 'no absences' |

| 971 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, What should you do during a typhoon? Today, Ember meets us at the Earth Science Museum with a big, empty backpack. He taps his yellow helmet and says, Safety first, fun always! Today, we will pack an emergency kit. An emergency kit is a bag of things you need when something big happens, like an earthquake or a typhoon. Riko asks, What goes inside? Ember smiles. The most important things come first. An emergency kit has water, food, and a flashlight. Say it with me. An emergency kit has water, food, and a flashlight. Great! Haru picks up a big bottle of water. Wow, this is heavy! Ember nods. Water is heavy, but we need a lot of it. Let's read Ember's checklist. "My Emergency Kit Checklist. Water: about three liters for each person for one day. Keep enough for at least three days. Food that keeps for a long time, like crackers and canned food. A flashlight and extra batteries. A radio, so you can hear the news. A first-aid kit for small cuts. A whistle, masks, gloves, and a warm jacket. Put copies of important papers in a waterproof bag. Check your kit often!" How much water does one person need for one day? About three liters. Why do we need a radio? So we can hear the news. Kiko packs the crackers and the canned food. Birimo tries on the gloves and waves his hands. Look, I'm ready for anything! Ember laughs. Don't forget the whistle, Birimo! Now, phonics. Proof means protects against, or strong against. Water plus proof is waterproof. A waterproof bag keeps the rain out. Wind plus proof is windproof. A windproof jacket keeps out the cold wind. Leak plus proof is leakproof. A leakproof bottle never drips, so your kit stays dry. Remember, we write each one as one word, with no space. Next time, we will visit a special safety house and look for the exits! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, What should you do during a typhoon? | 976 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, If there's an earthquake, we will go to Hilltop Park. Our meeting place is the big tree. Today, we visit the Island School. The children here are very friendly, and they invite us to join their evacuation drill. A drill is practice, so nobody needs to worry. It helps our bodies remember what to do. Ember stands at the front of the classroom in his yellow helmet. He says, When the ground shakes, protect your head first. Drop down low. Cover your head. Get under a desk and hold on. Say it with me. Get under a desk and hold on. Great job! Hold on means hold the leg of the desk tightly, so the desk stays over you. Let's read the poster on the classroom wall. "When the Ground Shakes. Drop down onto your hands and knees. Get under a desk and cover your head and neck. Hold on to the desk legs until the shaking stops. Stay away from windows and tall shelves. If there is no desk, cover your head with your bag or your arms. Stay where you are until your teacher tells you to move." What do you hold on to? The desk legs. What can you use if there is no desk? Your bag or your arms. Then a bell rings. This is a drill! We all drop, cover, and hold on. Birimo is too big for the desk, so he covers his head with his arms. Good thinking, Birimo! Now, phonics. Today, let's listen to lists. In a list, your voice goes up on each item and down on the last one. Drop, up. Cover, up. And hold on, down. We need water, up. Food, up. And a flashlight, down. Bring a radio, up. Batteries, up. And a whistle, down. Next time, we will practice moving out of the school slowly and quietly, with four important rules! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, If there's an earthquake, we will go to Hilltop Park. Our meeting place is the big tree. Today, we visit the Island School. The children here are very friendly, and they invite us to join their evacuation drill. A drill is practice, so nobody needs to worry. It helps our bodies remember what to do. Ember stands at the front of the classroom in his yellow helmet. He says, When the ground shakes, protect your head first. Drop down low. Cover your head. Get under a desk and hold on. Say it with me. Get under a desk and hold on. Great job! Hold on means hold the leg of the desk tightly, so the desk stays over you. Let's read the poster on the classroom wall. "When the Ground Shakes. Drop down onto your hands and knees. Get under a desk and cover your head and neck. Hold on to the desk legs until the shaking stops. Stay away from windows and tall shelves. If there is no desk, cover your head with your bag or your arms. Stay where you are until your teacher tells you to move." What do you hold on to? The desk legs. What can you use if there is no desk? Your bag or your arms. Then a bell rings. This is a drill! We all drop, cover, and hold on. All the desks are taken, so Birimo covers his head with his arms. Good thinking, Birimo! Now, phonics. Today, let's listen to lists. In a list, your voice goes up on each item and down on the last one. Drop, up. Cover, up. And hold on, down. We need water, up. Food, up. And a flashlight, down. Bring a radio, up. Batteries, up. And a whistle, down. Next time, we will practice moving out of the school slowly and quietly, with four important rules! | A+B | Birimo is the small one of the cast, so 'too big for the desk' contradicts him; 'All the desks are taken' also leads straight into 'What should we do if there's no desk?' |

| 978 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Stay calm. Follow your teacher. Today, Ember gives us six cards. Each card has one step on it, but they are all mixed up! Our job is to put them in the right order. Riko reads the first card. Get under your desk. That's easy. It's the first step! Kiko picks up the next card. Wait until the shaking stops. Then Haru asks, And after that? Can we go out right away? Ember says, As soon as the shaking stops, go outside carefully. Say it with me. As soon as the shaking stops, go outside carefully. Super! As soon as means right after something happens. But remember, carefully means we listen to the teacher and walk with our class. Let's read the cards in order. "After the Shaking. First, get under your desk and hold on. Wait until the shaking stops. As soon as the shaking stops, listen to your teacher. Line up at the door and put on your helmet or hood. Then, walk down the stairs with your class. Don't use the elevator. Go to the playground and stand in your class line. Your teacher will check that everyone is there." Where do you go after the stairs? To the playground. Who checks that everyone is there? The teacher. Haru checks the order. So, first we hide, and then we wait, right? Ember nods. That's right! You're great at this! Now, phonics. More lists! Remember, up, up, and down. Stay calm, up. Listen, up. And follow your teacher, down. Check the doors, up. The windows, up. And the gas, down. | 982 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, How can we help? We can carry water. Today, Grandma Tomo takes us for a walk through Harbor Town. She says, On our island, neighbors look after each other. After strong wind or a big shake, we visit the older people and the families next door. We knock on the door and ask kind questions. Soon, we see an old man sitting on a bench with a big bag of oranges. Grandma Tomo bends down, smiles, and says, Good morning! Are you OK? Do you need anything? Say it with me. Are you OK? Do you need anything? Great! The man smiles back. I'm fine, but this bag is a little heavy. Kiko asks, Can I help you carry that? Thank you. That's very kind. Let's read Grandma Tomo's Kind Words Card. "Kind Words for Neighbors. Smile and say hello first. Speak slowly and clearly so that older people can hear you. Ask, Are you OK? Do you need anything? Then listen carefully to the answer. Always go with an adult, and never go into a house alone. If someone needs help, tell an adult right away. Small, kind words can make a big difference. A neighbor who feels lonely may feel happy all day." Why should you speak slowly and clearly? So that older people can hear you. What should you do if someone needs help? Tell an adult right away. Kiko and Ms. Emma carry the oranges to the man's gate. He gives each of us an orange. Thank you, kind neighbors! Now, phonics. Ject means throw. Ob means toward. Object. An object is a thing in front of you, like an orange. Objection. If you have an objection, you say, I don't agree. Interject. Inter means between. When you interject, you put a few words in while someone is talking. Excuse me, may I interject? Next time, we will go to the harbor and help travelers who don't speak Japanese. | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, How can we help? We can carry water. Today, Grandma Tomo takes us for a walk through Harbor Town. She says, On our island, neighbors look after each other. After strong wind or a big shake, we visit the older people and the families next door. We knock on the door and ask kind questions. Soon, we see an old man sitting on a bench with a big bag of oranges. Grandma Tomo bends down, smiles, and says, Good morning! Are you OK? Do you need anything? Say it with me. Are you OK? Do you need anything? Great! The man smiles back. I'm fine, but this bag is a little heavy. Kiko asks, Can I help you carry that? Thank you. That's very kind. Let's read Grandma Tomo's Kind Words Card. "Kind Words for Neighbors. Smile and say hello first. Speak slowly and clearly so that older people can hear you. Ask, Are you OK? Do you need anything? Then listen carefully to the answer. Always go with an adult, and never go into a house alone. If someone needs help, tell an adult right away. Small, kind words can make a big difference. A neighbor who feels lonely may feel happy all day." Why should you speak slowly and clearly? So that older people can hear you. What should you do if someone needs help? Tell an adult right away. Kiko and I carry the oranges to the man's gate. He gives each of us an orange. Thank you, kind neighbors! Now, phonics. Ject means throw. Ob means toward. Object. An object is a thing in front of you, like an orange. Objection. If you have an objection, you say, I don't agree. Interject. Inter means between. When you interject, you put a few words in while someone is talking. Excuse me, may I interject? Next time, we will go to the harbor and help travelers who don't speak Japanese. | objective | Ms. Emma narrates her own story (the next sentence says 'each of us'); first person like the block-wide fix |

| 987 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Be ready! Know your exits! Today, we're back at our little inn in Harbor Town. On the back of our room door, there's a card with a small map and some steps. Ember taps the card with his tail. When you stay in a new place, read this first! Ms. Emma nods and says, Read the instructions. What should you do first? Say it with me. Read the instructions. What should you do first? Great! Let's read the card together. "Evacuation Instructions. Step one: When the alarm rings, stay calm and listen to the staff. Step two: Find the arrows on the floor plan. The nearest exit is at the end of the hall. Step three: Use the stairs. Never use the elevator during an evacuation. Step four: Walk, don't run, and don't push. Step five: Go to the meeting place in front of the inn and wait for guidance from the staff." What should you do first? Stay calm and listen to the staff. Do we use the stairs or the elevator? The stairs, always. Then Birimo asks, Is the shelter near or far? Ember points at the map. The Island School gym is just a short walk away. Haru opens the door and checks the hall. Look, the exit sign is right there! Now we know the way. Now, phonics. Let's review. Interrupted. Inter means between, and rupt means break. When someone breaks in while you are talking, you are interrupted. Guidance. Guide, take away the e, and add a, n, c, e. Childproof. A childproof cap is hard for small children to open. Is the shelter near or far? Near, up. Or far, down. Injection. In means into, and ject means throw. Next time, we will write our own safety promises. | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Be ready! | 991 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Know it, prepare for it, and help each other. Today, we follow the light of the Safety Lantern to the small airport on the Island of Fire. Look at its big white roof. It looks like two huge wings! That's why people call it the Airport of Wings. Birimo opens his map. There's a little wings mark right here! Then a young albatross comes over to us. She wears a blue scarf and a captain's cap. Hello! I'm Skye, the junior guide of this airport. Every trip is a story! She points to the tall windows. Planes are taking off one after another. Skye says, From here, planes leave for every corner of the world. Say it with me. Planes leave for every corner of the world. Great! Ms. Emma smiles and holds up her travel pouch. Your passports are safe in here, she says. We're going on a trip abroad together! Skye gives each of us a small blue book. Let's read the first page. "Welcome to the Wing Stamp Book! This is not a passport. It is a book for your travel story. There are six empty pages inside. Each time you finish one part of your trip, one page will be filled. Use polite words, help your friends, and always stay with your group. When all six pages are full, a big surprise is waiting for you. Have a wonderful trip, and remember, every trip is a story!" How many empty pages are there? Six. Is the Wing Stamp Book a passport? No, it isn't. It's a book for our travel story. Now, phonics. Look at the departures board. We read flight numbers in small parts. Flight 205 is flight two oh five. Zero often sounds like oh. Flight 318 is flight three eighteen. Flight 760 is flight seven sixty. Flight 1120 is flight eleven twenty. Our plane is Flight 205! Next time, we will go to the check-in counter with our bags! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Know it, prepare for it, and help each other. | 992 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Planes leave for every corner of the world. Today, we pull our suitcases to the check-in counter. There's a short line, so we wait our turn. Then a friendly clerk in a green jacket waves to us. Ms. Emma steps up to the counter with all of us and says, Good morning. I'd like to check in, please. Say it with me. I'd like to check in, please. Great! Before we go on, let's read the sign on the counter. "Welcome to the Check-In Counter. Check-in for Flight 205 opens at 7:15 and closes at 10:00. Please have your passport ready. Put your bags on the scale one at a time. We will put a baggage tag on each bag, so it can find its way to the right plane. Keep medicine and important things in your small bag. After check-in, please go to the security check. Have a wonderful flight!" What time does check-in open? At 7:15. What goes on each bag? A baggage tag. The clerk asks, May I see your passports, please? Ms. Emma takes our passports out of her travel pouch. Here you are. Then the clerk asks, How many bags are you checking? Two bags, please. The bags go on the scale, and the clerk puts a baggage tag on each one. Bye-bye, bags! See you later! Birimo tries to climb onto the scale, too. Oh, Birimo! You're not a bag! Everyone laughs, even the clerk. Now, phonics. Let's read times. Say the hour, and then the minutes. 10:45 is ten forty-five. That's when our plane leaves. 6:05 is six oh five. The zero sounds like oh. 12:30 is twelve thirty. 7:15 is seven fifteen. Listen carefully. Fifteen is strong at the end. Fifty is strong at the beginning. Next time, we will choose our seats. | 993 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I'd like to check in, please. We are still at the check-in counter. Our bags have their tags, and now it's time to choose our seats. The clerk looks at Haru and asks, Would you like a window seat or an aisle seat? Say it with me. Would you like a window seat or an aisle seat? Great! An aisle is the long, narrow path between the seats. Haru doesn't need to think. A window seat, please! I want to see the clouds. Sure. Your seat is 14A. Kiko says, I'd like an aisle seat, please. Then I can stand up and stretch my legs. Riko is happy in the middle, between her two friends. Ms. Emma asks, Can we sit together? The clerk smiles. Of course. You're all in rows 14 and 15. Ms. Emma will sit right behind the children, in 15B. Then the clerk prints our boarding passes. Let's read Haru's boarding pass. "Boarding Pass. Passenger: Haru. Flight: 205. From: the Airport of Wings. To: Skyport International Airport. Gate: 23B. Boarding time: 10:15. Departure time: 10:45. Seat: 14A, window. Seats A and F are next to the windows. Please be at your gate by boarding time. The gate closes fifteen minutes before departure. Keep this boarding pass with your passport until you are on the plane. Thank you, and enjoy your flight!" What is Haru's seat? 14A, a window seat. What time does boarding start? At 10:15. Birimo reads his boarding pass. Seat 15A! A window seat, too! He does a happy little dance. Now, phonics. Seat numbers are a number and a letter. Seat 14A is seat fourteen A. Seat 32F is seat thirty-two F. Seat 8C is seat eight C. Seat 21D is seat twenty-one D. Be careful with fourteen and forty. Fourteen is strong at the end. Forty is strong at the beginning. Next time, we will listen to the airport announcements very carefully! | Hello, everyone! | 994 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Would you like a window seat or an aisle seat? Today, we go through the security check. We put our small bags on the belt, and they ride slowly through a big machine. Then we walk through a gate, one by one, while Ms. Emma watches us. Beep! Oh, it's just Birimo's metal water bottle. A staff member checks it, smiles, and gives it back. No problem! After security, we sit in the waiting area by the big windows. Skye says, Airports are full of announcements, so listen carefully for your flight number. Let's read the announcements we heard this morning. "Good morning, passengers. Flight 318 to Sunny Bay is now boarding at gate 41C. Passengers on Flight 760 to Pine Island, your gate has changed. Please go to gate 15. Passengers on Flight 205 to Skyport International Airport, please have your boarding pass and passport ready. Families with small children may board first. Flight 205 leaves at 10:45. Thank you for waiting." Where is Flight 318 boarding? At gate 41C. Who may board Flight 205 first? Families with small children. Then a bell rings. Ding, dong! Flight 205 is now boarding at gate 23B. That's us! Say it with me. Flight 205 is now boarding at gate 23B. Great! Boarding means getting on the plane. Haru jumps up. Did you hear that? Riko nods. Yes! That's our flight! Ms. Emma says, Let's get our things. Stay close to me, everyone. Now, phonics. Gate numbers are like seat numbers. Gate 23B is gate twenty-three B. Gate 41C is gate forty-one C. Gate 102 is gate one oh two. The zero sounds like oh. Gate 15 is gate fifteen. Fifteen is strong at the end, so don't mix it up with fifty. Next time, we will look for gate 23B and say goodbye to Skye! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Would you like a window seat or an aisle seat? Today, we go through the security check. | 995 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Flight 205 is now boarding at gate 23B. We pick up our bags and walk down the long hall. But wait. There are two signs. One arrow says Gates 1 to 20. The other arrow says Gates 21 to 30, but it points up! Where is gate 23B? Ms. Emma sees an airport staff member in a yellow vest. We all go over together, and Riko asks politely. Excuse me. Do you know where gate 23B is? Say it with me. Excuse me. Do you know where gate 23B is? Great! It's a softer way to say, Where is gate 23B? The staff member smiles. Yes! Go up the stairs and turn left. It's next to the big window. Riko says, Thank you so much! At gate 23B, Skye is waiting for us. She opens our Wing Stamp Books and presses a little stamp on the first page. Let's read the note she wrote next to it. "Dear friends, today you checked in, chose your seats, listened to the announcements, and asked the way politely. You did a wonderful job! Here is your first stamp, a little blue plane. On the plane, fasten your seatbelts and stay close to Ms. Emma. I'm going to fly across the sea, too, so I'll see you again soon. Every trip is a story, and yours is just beginning! Love, Skye" What is the first stamp? A little blue plane. What will Skye do? She'll fly across the sea and see us again. Goodbye, Skye! She waves her long wings. Have a great flight! I'll fly to meet you! Now, phonics. Let's review our airport numbers. Flight 205 is flight two oh five. 10:45 is ten forty-five. Seat 14A is seat fourteen A. Gate 23B is gate twenty-three B. | 996 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Excuse me. Do you know where gate 23B is? Today, we are on Flight 205! Haru sits by the window in 14A, Riko is in 14B, and Kiko is on the aisle in 14C. Birimo and Ms. Emma are right behind them. Ding! The seatbelt sign is on, so we fasten our seatbelts. Click! The plane goes faster and faster down the runway, and then we're in the air! Haru's ears feel strange. Ms. Emma says, Try yawning or chewing. That helps. Soon a kind flight attendant comes down the aisle with a cart. Kiko is thirsty, so she asks, Could I have some water, please? Say it with me. Could I have some water, please? Great! The flight attendant smiles. Of course. Here you are. Riko feels a little cold. Could I have a blanket, please? Then the pilot makes an announcement. Let's read it. "Good afternoon, everyone. This is your captain speaking. Welcome aboard Flight 205. We are now flying high above the sea. The weather is clear, and the visibility is excellent, so please look out of your window. You can see small green islands below. In a few minutes, our flight attendants will serve drinks and a light lunch. When the seatbelt sign is on, please stay in your seat with your seatbelt fastened. | 997 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Could I have some water, please? After a long flight, we land at Skyport International Airport. It's huge! There are moving walkways, bright signs, and travelers from all over the world. Ms. Emma says, Stay together, everyone. First, we go to passport control. Let's read the sign on the wall. "Welcome to Skyport International Airport. All visitors, please go to Passport Control. Have your passport ready. Families may go to the officer together, and children should stay with their adult. The officer will check your passport and may ask you a few simple questions, for example, why you are visiting and how long you will stay. After that, collect your bags and go through Customs. Enjoy your stay!" What should children do? Stay with their adult. Where do you go after passport control? To Customs. Now it's our turn. We walk up to the desk together. A friendly officer smiles at us. Hello! What's the purpose of your visit? Ms. Emma answers, Sightseeing. Say it with me. What's the purpose of your visit? Sightseeing. Great! Purpose means reason. Sightseeing means visiting famous and beautiful places. The officer asks, How long will you stay? For five days. Where are you staying? At the Seagull Hotel in Brightwater. Then the officer looks at Kiko and asks, Is this your first visit? Kiko answers, Yes, it is! The officer laughs and says, Welcome! Have a wonderful time! Now, phonics. Vis means see. | 998 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, What's the purpose of your visit? Sightseeing. We are in a new country now, but our trip isn't over yet. Brightwater is a city by the sea, so we need to take one more plane. This is called a connecting flight. Our next plane is a domestic flight. Domestic means inside the same country. But Skyport is so big! Ms. Emma walks to the transfer desk with all of us and asks, How do I transfer to my next flight? Say it with me. How do I transfer to my next flight? Great! The man at the desk smiles. Follow the green Connecting Flights signs, and check the departures board for your gate. Let's read the departures board. "Departures. Flight 1120 to Brightwater leaves at 4:20 from gate 41C. It is on time. Flight 760 to Green Valley leaves at 4:50 from gate 15. It is delayed. Flight 540 to Maple Hill leaves at 5:10 from gate 12. It is on time. Passengers with connecting flights, please follow the green signs. Your gate may change, so please check this board again before you go. If you need help, ask at the transfer desk next to the escalators." When does Flight 1120 leave? At 4:20. What should you do before you go to your gate? Check the board again. We follow the green signs. Haru reads every one out loud. Connecting Flights, this way! Birimo marches like a leader, but he stays right next to Ms. Emma. Now, phonics. Vis means see. Television. Tele means far, so a television shows pictures from far away. The departures board is on a big screen like a television. Invisible. In means not, so invisible means impossible to see. Supervise. Super means over. Ms. Emma supervises us. She watches over us and keeps us safe. Next time, we will change some money while we wait for our plane! | Hello, everyone! Ms. Emma answers, I'd like to change some yen, please. Say it with me. I'd like to change some yen, please. Great! Before the clerk starts, let's read the sign on the counter. "Currency Exchange. Welcome to Skyport! Here, you can change your money into dollars. Today's exchange rate is on the board above the counter. The rate is a little different every day. Please count your money carefully before you leave the counter, and keep your receipt. If you need small coins for buses or snacks, just ask. Thank you, and have a lovely stay!" Where is today's exchange rate? On the board above the counter. What should you do before you leave? Count your money carefully. The clerk gives Ms. Emma some bills. Ms. Emma asks, Could we have some coins, too? Certainly. Here you are. Then Ms. Emma says, Let's count it together. Haru counts the bills. Ten, twenty, thirty dollars! Kiko counts the shiny coins. Birimo holds a coin up to the light. It has a seagull on it! Ms. Emma puts the money in her pouch and zips it up. Now, phonics. Vis means see. Revise. Re means again. | 1001 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, We've arrived! This is our 1,000th lesson! Now it's evening in Brightwater. Skye waves goodbye and flies off to visit a friend by the harbor. Ms. Emma takes us to our hotel near the sea. It's called the Seagull Hotel, and a big wooden seagull hangs above the door. Inside, the lobby smells like fresh flowers. A man in a green jacket smiles at us from the front desk. His name is Mr. Lee, and he is always smiling. Before the trip, Ms. Emma made our reservation, and she put it under Haru's name. So Haru steps up to the desk with Ms. Emma and says, Good evening. I have a reservation under the name Haru. Say it with me. I have a reservation under the name Haru. Great! Mr. Lee checks his computer and says, Yes, here it is! Five guests, one adult and four children. Then he gives us a welcome card. Let's read the welcome card. "Welcome to the Seagull Hotel! Your room is 512 on the fifth floor. Breakfast is from seven to ten in the Harbor Room on the first floor. Check-out time is eleven in the morning. Please take your room key with you when you go out. If you need anything, call the front desk any time, day or night. We hope you enjoy your stay in Brightwater!" What is our room number? 512. When is breakfast? From seven to ten. Birimo wants to guard the key all night, but Ms. Emma keeps it safe in her pouch. Now, phonics. Today's words have double letters, but we say the sound only once. Accommodation means a place to stay. It has two c's and two m's. Address has two d's and two s's. Necessary has one c and two s's. Next time, we will go up to our room and ask for things very politely! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, We've arrived! This is our 1,000th lesson! | 1002 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I have a reservation under the name Haru. Today, we are in Room 512. It has two big beds and a window over a busy street. The room feels a little warm and stuffy. Ms. Emma is unpacking her backpack. She turns to Haru and says, Would you mind opening the window? Haru says, Not at all! He opens it, and fresh sea air comes in. Would you mind（～して もらえますか）is a very polite way to ask someone to do something. After would you mind, we use an ing word: opening, closing, helping. Here is the funny part. Mind means feel bothered. So when you are happy to help, you say, Not at all! It means, It's no trouble at all. Say it with me. Would you mind opening the window? Wonderful! Let's read the room guide on the desk. "Room Guide. Coffee and tea are free. You can find them next to the kettle. Extra pillows and blankets are in the closet. If you need more towels, please call the front desk. Please close the window when you leave the room, and turn off the lights to save energy. Quiet time starts at ten at night. Thank you for being a kind guest!" Where are the extra pillows? In the closet. When does quiet time start? At ten at night. Kiko opens the closet, but there is only one extra pillow, and Birimo is already hugging it. Kiko smiles and asks, Birimo, would you mind sharing? Birimo says, Not at all! Now, phonics. More double letters today. Pillow has two l's. P, i, l, l, o, w. Coffee has two f's and two e's. C, o, f, f, e, e. Occupied means someone is using it, like a seat or a bathroom. It has two c's and one p. Next time, we will ask Mr. Lee how to get to the nearest station! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I have a reservation under the name Haru. Today, we are in Room 512. It has two big beds and a window over a busy street. The room feels a little warm and stuffy. I'm unpacking my backpack. I turn to Haru and say, Would you mind opening the window? Haru says, Not at all! He opens it, and fresh sea air comes in. Would you mind（～して もらえますか）is a very polite way to ask someone to do something. After would you mind, we use an ing word: opening, closing, helping. Here is the funny part. Mind means feel bothered. So when you are happy to help, you say, Not at all! It means, It's no trouble at all. Say it with me. Would you mind opening the window? Wonderful! Let's read the room guide on the desk. "Room Guide. Coffee and tea are free. You can find them next to the kettle. Extra pillows and blankets are in the closet. If you need more towels, please call the front desk. Please close the window when you leave the room, and turn off the lights to save energy. Quiet time starts at ten at night. Thank you for being a kind guest!" Where are the extra pillows? In the closet. When does quiet time start? At ten at night. Kiko opens the closet, but there is only one extra pillow, and Birimo is already hugging it. Kiko smiles and asks, Birimo, would you mind sharing? Birimo says, Not at all! Now, phonics. More double letters today. Pillow has two l's. P, i, l, l, o, w. Coffee has two f's and two e's. C, o, f, f, e, e. Occupied means someone is using it, like a seat or a bathroom. It has two c's and one p. Next time, we will ask Mr. Lee how to get to the nearest station! | A+C (block rule; group reports L981-990, L991-1000, L1001-1010) | Ms. Emma reads her own teacherScript; her own narration said "Ms. Emma says / takes us / her pouch". -> I / my / me, same as say/subtitles and as L991-1000 (group). | 1003 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Would you mind opening the window? This morning, after a big breakfast in the Harbor Room, we want to explore Brightwater. Ms. Emma says the train is the easiest way. But where is the station? Ms. Emma says, Let's ask at the front desk. We walk to the lobby together. We could just ask, Where is the station? That's fine, but there is a softer, more polite way. Listen. Where is the station? becomes, Do you know where the station is? The verb is moves to the end. Where are the boats? becomes, Do you know where the boats are? Riko tries it. Excuse me. Do you know where the nearest station is? Say it with me. Do you know where the nearest station is? Excellent! Another polite way is, Could you tell me where the station is? Mr. Lee says, Of course! It's Clock Tower Station. It's a five-minute walk. He draws a little map for us. Let's read Mr. Lee's map card. "From the Seagull Hotel to Clock Tower Station. Go out of the front entrance and turn left. Walk straight along Harbor Street for two blocks. You will see a bakery on the corner. Turn right at the bakery. The station is at the end of that street, right next to the tall clock tower. You can't miss it! It takes about five minutes on foot." Which way do we turn at the entrance? Left. Where is the station? Next to the clock tower. Kiko asks, Could you tell me when the next train to the harbor leaves? Mr. Lee checks and says, At nine thirty. Ms. Emma says, Thank you! Stay close to me, everyone. Now, phonics. Tomorrow has one m and two r's. Different has two f's. D, i, f, f, e, r, e, n, t. Arrive has two r's. Next time, we will ask Mr. Lee about a good place to eat dinner! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Would you mind opening the window? This morning, after a big breakfast in the Harbor Room, we want to explore Brightwater. I say the train is the easiest way. But where is the station? I say, Let's ask at the front desk. Tomorrow has one m and two r's. Different has two f's. D, i, f, f, e, r, e, n, t. Arrive has two r's. | 1004 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Do you know where the nearest station is? We spent the day at the harbor with Ms. Emma, and now we are very hungry! Birimo's tummy rumbles like a little thunderstorm. Ms. Emma says, Let's ask Mr. Lee. He knows the city very well. When you want a good idea from someone who knows a place, you can say, Could you recommend a good restaurant? To recommend something means to say, This one is good. You should try it! Haru tries it. Excuse me. Could you recommend a good restaurant? Say it with me. Could you recommend a good restaurant? Great! Mr. Lee smiles and asks, What kind of food do you like? Riko says, We love fish! Mr. Lee says, Then how about the Little Anchor? It's a fish restaurant by the river. He gives us a page from the hotel's city guide. Let's read the guide. "The Little Anchor. This small family restaurant sits right by the river, near the old stone bridge. The fish comes fresh from the harbor every morning. Try the grilled fish with lemon, or the famous fish soup. There is a children's menu with fish and chips and fruit juice. It is open from five to nine in the evening. Ask for a table by the window and watch the boats go by!" Where is the restaurant? By the river. What time does it open? At five in the evening. Kiko says, Thank you for the great idea, Mr. Lee! Mr. Lee says, My pleasure. I'm sure you'll love it. We decide to go there another day. Now, phonics. Recommend has one c and two m's. Excellent has two l's. Suggest has two g's. S, u, g, g, e, s, t. To suggest means to give an idea, like, I suggest the fish soup! Next time, we will practice all our hotel English, and we will need umbrellas! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Do you know where the nearest station is? | 1005 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Could you recommend a good restaurant? Today, we are practicing all our hotel English. Birimo says, I'm playing Mr. Lee! Welcome to the Seagull Hotel! We take turns. Haru checks in with his reservation. Riko asks for an extra pillow. Kiko asks where the nearest station is. Then, oh no! Dark clouds are rolling in, and it starts to rain. We want to walk to the River Market with Ms. Emma, but we don't have umbrellas. Ms. Emma says, Many hotels lend umbrellas to guests. Let's ask politely. Kiko goes to the front desk with Ms. Emma and says, I'd like to borrow an umbrella. Could you lend me one? Say it with me. I'd like to borrow an umbrella. Could you lend me one? Super! Borrow means you take something and give it back later. Lend means you give something, and it comes back to you. Mr. Lee points to a sign. Let's read the sign. "Rainy Day? We can help! Seagull Hotel guests may borrow umbrellas from the front desk for free. Large umbrellas are blue, and small umbrellas for children are yellow. Please write your room number on the list when you borrow one. If you need a raincoat, just ask. Bring the umbrellas back to the front desk before you check out. Enjoy Brightwater, rain or shine!" What color are the children's umbrellas? Yellow. When do we bring them back? Before we check out. Then Skye lands by the door, shaking rain from her wings. She says, Every trip is a story, and today's story is about kind words! She opens the Wing Stamp Book and presses our third stamp onto the page. Now, phonics. Let's review. Accommodation has two c's and two m's. Recommend has one c and two m's. Visitor. Vis means see. Gate 41C is gate forty-one C, not fourteen C. Next time, Ms. Emma will tell us a surprising secret about her suitcase! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Could you recommend a good restaurant? Today, we are practicing all our hotel English. Birimo says, I'm playing Mr. Lee! Welcome to the Seagull Hotel! We take turns. Haru checks in with his reservation. Riko asks for an extra pillow. Kiko asks where the nearest station is. Then, oh no! | 1006 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I'd like to borrow an umbrella. Could you lend me one? This morning, Ms. Emma sits down with us in the lobby. She says, I have something to tell you. On the day we arrived, my suitcase didn't come out at baggage claim. I didn't want you to worry on our special day, so I used my backpack. Haru says, Oh! That's why you wore the same green sweater every day! Ms. Emma laughs. She says, Today, let's go to the Baggage Service desk at Brightwater Airport together. When your bag doesn't come, don't get upset. Just explain clearly and politely. My suitcase didn't arrive. Say it with me. My suitcase didn't arrive. Good! At the airport, a kind woman at the desk listens carefully. She asks, What does it look like? Ms. Emma says, It's big and blue, with a yellow ribbon on the handle. It has a name tag, too. The woman says, We're very sorry. Could you fill out this form, please? Let's read the form. "Baggage Service Form. Flight: 1120 from Skyport. Item: one large suitcase. Color: blue. Special marks: a yellow ribbon on the handle and a name tag. Inside: clothes, shoes, a hairbrush, and a travel guide. Please send the bag to the Seagull Hotel in Brightwater. We apologize for the trouble. We will call your hotel as soon as we find your bag." What color is the suitcase? Blue. What is on the handle? A yellow ribbon. That night, the phone in our room rings. Mr. Lee says, Good news! Your suitcase is here at the front desk! We all cheer. Now, phonics. Today's words end in ize, and they are all verbs. Apology, drop the y, add ize. Apologize. Real, add ize. Realize means suddenly know that something is true. Summary, drop the y, add ize. Summarize means tell the main points. Next time, our room gets very hot, and something is broken! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I'd like to borrow an umbrella. Could you lend me one? This morning, I sit down with you in the lobby. I say, I have something to tell you. | 1007 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, My suitcase didn't arrive. Good news! Ms. Emma has her blue suitcase back. But today is the hottest day of our trip. After lunch, we come back to Room 512 to rest. Phew! It's like an oven in here! Riko presses the button on the remote control. The air conditioner makes a strange rattling noise, and only warm air comes out. Ms. Emma says, Let's call the front desk. Riko, would you like to try? I'll be right next to you. To explain a problem politely, start with I'm afraid. I'm afraid（ざんねんですが）makes your words soft and kind. Riko picks up the room phone and says, Hello, this is Room 512. I'm afraid the air conditioner is broken. Say it with me. I'm afraid the air conditioner is broken. Perfect! Riko adds, It's making a strange noise, and it's still hot. Mr. Lee says, I'm so sorry. We'll send someone right away. While we wait, Kiko finds a card next to the TV. Let's read the card. "How to Use Your Air Conditioner. Press the red button to turn it on or off. Use the up and down arrows to make the room warmer or cooler. Keep the windows closed when it is on. If the air conditioner does not work, please do not try to fix it yourself. Call the front desk, and a member of our repair team will come to help you." What does the red button do? It turns the air conditioner on or off. What should you do if it doesn't work? Call the front desk. Soon, Sam the repair man arrives with his toolbox. Birimo says, I'm playing his helper! and holds the flashlight. Now, phonics. More ize verbs! Modern, add ize. Modernize means make something new. Energy, drop the y, add ize. Energize means give energy. Final, add ize. Finalize means finish the last details. Next time, Sam has some news, and we need a new plan! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, My suitcase didn't arrive. Good news! Ms. Emma has her blue suitcase back. Mr. Lee gives Ms. Emma a new key and a small card. Let's read the card. "Room Change. Dear Guests, we are very sorry about the air conditioner in Room 512. Your new room is Room 803 on the eighth floor. It has a large window with a view of the Clock Tower. At night, the tower lights up in gold. Our porter will help you carry your bags. | 1009 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Could you give us a different room instead? We love our new room. But this morning, Ms. Emma is looking through her backpack. Then her jacket. Then her suitcase. She says calmly, Everyone, I can't find my passport. Your passports are safe in the travel pouch, but mine isn't there. Then she adds, Don't worry. When something is lost, the first thing to do is stay calm. If you ever lose something important on a trip, tell an adult right away. I'm the adult, so I'll ask at the front desk. I lost my passport. What should I do? Say it with me. I lost my passport. What should I do? Well done! We check all our bags together, and then we all go down to the lobby. Mr. Lee listens kindly and asks, Where did you last use it? Ms. Emma remembers. At the Baggage Service desk at the airport! I showed it there for the form. Mr. Lee says, I'll call the airport for you. While he calls, he gives us a card. Let's read the card. "Lost Your Passport? Don't panic. First, look carefully in your bags and pockets. Next, think about the last place you used it. Then, tell the hotel front desk. We can make phone calls for you. If you still cannot find it, an adult should go to the police and then contact their country's embassy. Children, always keep your passport with a grown-up you trust." Who should go to the embassy? An adult. Who should children keep their passports with? A grown-up they trust. Good news! The airport has it. It's safe at the Baggage Service desk. We all go to the airport with Ms. Emma and pick it up. Now, phonics. Memory, drop the y, add ize. Memorize. Sympathy, drop the y, add ize. Sympathize means understand how someone feels. | 1010 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I lost my passport. What should I do? Ms. Emma's passport is back, safe in the travel pouch with ours. We had a late suitcase, a broken air conditioner, and a missing passport. But every problem was solved, because we stayed calm and polite, and kind people helped us. Today, we are making thank-you cards. When someone helps you, you can say, Thank you for your help. And to make it extra warm, add, That's very kind of you. Say it with me. Thank you for your help. That's very kind of you. Beautiful! First, we play Problem Solved! Birimo says, I'm playing Mr. Lee! Haru says, I'm afraid the shower is broken. Birimo answers, I'm so sorry. We'll fix it right away. Riko says, Could we have a different room instead? Then Kiko reads her card to the real Mr. Lee. Let's read Kiko's card. "Dear Mr. Lee, thank you for your help this week. You gave us a cool new room with a view of the Clock Tower, and you called the airport to find Ms. Emma's passport. You always smile, even when there is a problem. That's very kind of you. We will never forget the Seagull Hotel. Love, Kiko, Haru, Riko, and Birimo." What can we see from our new room? The Clock Tower. Who did Mr. Lee call? The airport. We also make a card for the Baggage Service staff. Then, at twelve thirty, Skye flies in through the lobby door. She says, Every trip is a story, and this one has a happy ending! She gives us our fourth stamp. Now, phonics. Let's review. Apology, drop the y, add ize. Apologize. Real, add ize. Realize. Luggage has two g's. L, u, g, g, a, g, e. Vision. Vis means see, so vision is how well you can see. 12:30 is twelve thirty. Next time, we will explore Brightwater and ask someone to take our picture! | Hello, everyone! | 1011 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Thank you for your help. That's very kind of you. Today, it's time to explore Brightwater! The sun is shining after yesterday's rain. Ms. Emma walks with us along the river, and Skye flies above us in her blue scarf. She calls down, Every trip is a story! Soon we reach Clock Tower Square. The tall Clock Tower stands in the middle, next to a fountain and a statue of a seagull. We want a picture of all of us together. Ms. Emma smiles at a kind woman with a map and says, Excuse me. Could you take our picture? The woman says, Of course! Say it with me. Excuse me. Could you take our picture? Great! Remember, we always ask with Ms. Emma right next to us. Let's read the sign in the square. "Welcome to Clock Tower Square. The Clock Tower was built more than two hundred years ago. Its bell rings every hour, from eight in the morning to eight at night. Please stay on the pavement and do not climb into the fountain. The underground station is across the bridge. Please join the queue at the ticket machines and check the timetable on the wall." When does the bell ring? Every hour, from eight in the morning to eight at night. Where is the underground station? Across the bridge. Just then, the bell rings. Bong, bong, bong! Click! The woman takes our picture at the perfect moment. Now, phonics. Today, we learn British and American words. Underground. In the UK, people say underground. In the US, people say subway. Sidewalk. In the US, people say sidewalk. In the UK, people say pavement. Queue. Q, u, e, u, e. It sounds just like the letter Q! In the US, people say line. Timetable. In the US, people say schedule. Both are correct English. Next time, we will go to the fish restaurant by the river and order lunch! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Thank you for your help. That's very kind of you. Today, it's time to explore Brightwater! The sun is shining after yesterday's rain. Ms. Emma walks with us along the river, and Skye flies above us in her blue scarf. | 1012 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Excuse me. Could you take our picture? Today, we're hungry after our long walk. Ms. Emma takes us to the fish restaurant by the river. Mr. Lee recommended it, remember? We sit at a table by the window, and we can see boats on the water. A friendly waiter comes over with glasses of water and says, Good afternoon! Riko smiles and asks, Could we have the menu, please? Say it with me. Could we have the menu, please? Great! We say we, because we are a group. Let's read the menu. "River Fish Restaurant. Today's Lunch. Fish and chips with peas, ten dollars. Grilled salmon with rice and salad, twelve dollars. Fish soup with warm bread, eight dollars. Children's plate, small fish and chips with apple juice, six dollars. Desserts. Chocolate cake, four dollars. Ice cream, three dollars. Every children's plate comes with a free biscuit. Please tell our staff if you have any allergies." How much is the fish soup? Eight dollars. What comes with every children's plate? A free biscuit. The waiter asks, What would you like? Haru says, I'd like the fish and chips, please. Birimo tries to order three ice creams, and Ms. Emma laughs. One is enough, Birimo! After lunch, Ms. Emma asks, Could we have the bill, please? Now, phonics. Today, let's learn more British and American words. Bill. In the UK, people say bill. In the US, people say check. Biscuit. In the UK, a biscuit is a sweet, flat snack. In the US, people call it a cookie. Candy. In the US, people say candy. In the UK, people say sweets. Trash. In the US, people say trash. In the UK, people say rubbish. Both are correct English. Next time, we will meet two friendly travelers at the hotel, one from the UK and one from the US! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Excuse me. Could you take our picture? | 1012 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Excuse me. Could you take our picture? Today, we're hungry after our long walk. Ms. Emma takes us to the fish restaurant by the river. Mr. Lee recommended it, remember? We sit at a table by the window, and we can see boats on the water. A friendly waiter comes over with glasses of water and says, Good afternoon! Riko smiles and asks, Could we have the menu, please? Say it with me. Could we have the menu, please? Great! We say we, because we are a group. Let's read the menu. "River Fish Restaurant. Today's Lunch. Fish and chips with peas, ten dollars. Grilled salmon with rice and salad, twelve dollars. Fish soup with warm bread, eight dollars. Children's plate, small fish and chips with apple juice, six dollars. Desserts. Chocolate cake, four dollars. Ice cream, three dollars. Every children's plate comes with a free cookie. Please tell our staff if you have any allergies." How much is the fish soup? Eight dollars. What comes with every children's plate? A free cookie. The waiter asks, What would you like? Haru says, I'd like the fish and chips, please. Birimo tries to order three ice creams, and Ms. Emma laughs. One is enough, Birimo! After lunch, Ms. Emma asks, Could we have the check, please? Now, phonics. Today, let's learn more British and American words. Bill. In the UK, people say bill. In the US, people say check. Biscuit. In the UK, a biscuit is a sweet, flat snack. In the US, people call it a cookie. Candy. In the US, people say candy. In the UK, people say sweets. Trash. In the US, people say trash. In the UK, people say rubbish. Both are correct English. Next time, we will meet two friendly travelers at the hotel, one from the UK and one from the US! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Excuse me. Could you take our picture? Today, we're hungry after our long walk. We go to the fish restaurant by the river. Mr. Lee recommended it, remember? We sit at a table by the window, and we can see boats on the water. A friendly waiter comes over with glasses of water and says, Good afternoon! Riko smiles and asks, Could we have the menu, please? Say it with me. Could we have the menu, please? Great! We say we, because we are a group. Let's read the menu. "River Fish Restaurant. Today's Lunch. Fish and chips with peas, ten dollars. Grilled salmon with rice and salad, twelve dollars. Fish soup with warm bread, eight dollars. Children's plate, small fish and chips with apple juice, six dollars. Desserts. Chocolate cake, four dollars. Ice cream, three dollars. Every children's plate comes with a free cookie. Please tell our staff if you have any allergies." How much is the fish soup? Eight dollars. What comes with every children's plate? A free cookie. The waiter asks, What would you like? Haru says, I'd like the fish and chips, please. Birimo tries to order three ice creams, and I laugh. One is enough, Birimo! After lunch, I ask, Could we have the check, please? Now, phonics. Today, let's learn more British and American words. Bill. In the UK, people say bill. In the US, people say check. Biscuit. In the UK, a biscuit is a sweet, flat snack. In the US, people call it a cookie. Candy. In the US, people say candy. In the UK, people say sweets. Trash. In the US, people say trash. In the UK, people say rubbish. Both are correct English. Next time, we will meet two friendly travelers at the hotel, one from the UK and one from the US! | A+C (block rule; group reports L981-990, L991-1000, L1001-1010) | Ms. Emma reads her own teacherScript; her own narration said "Ms. Emma says / takes us / her pouch". -> I / my / me, same as say/subtitles and as L991-1000 (group). | 1013 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Could we have the menu, please? This evening, we come back to the Seagull Hotel. Two friendly travelers are drinking tea in the lobby. Mrs. Brown is from the UK, and Mr. Green is from the US. They are on a trip, too! Mrs. Brown says, I'm on holiday with my sister. Mr. Green laughs and says, And I'm on vacation with my son. When it's time to go upstairs, Mrs. Brown says, Let's take the lift. Mr. Green says, You mean the elevator! Everyone laughs. Ms. Emma explains, English is spoken in many countries, and some words are different. In the UK, people say lift. Say it with me. In the UK, people say lift. Great! In the US, people say elevator. Both are correct English. Mrs. Brown shows us a little card she made for her grandson. Let's read the card. "Words from Two Countries. In the UK, we take the lift up to our room. In the US, people take the elevator. In the UK, we go on holiday in the summer. In the US, people go on vacation. We often eat biscuits with our tea, and in the US, people often eat cookies with milk. We use different words, but we can always understand each other with a smile." What do people in the US often eat with milk? Cookies. How can we always understand each other? With a smile. Birimo says, In Birimo Land, people say up box! Mr. Green laughs so hard that he almost drops his cup. Now, phonics. Today, let's compare four words. Lift and elevator. In the UK, people say lift. In the US, people say elevator. Elevator has a strong first part. EL, e, va, tor. Holiday and vacation. In the UK, people say holiday. In the US, people say vacation. Vacation has a strong middle part. Va, CA, tion. Next time, we will go to the City Museum and buy tickets for everyone! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Could we have the menu, please? After lunch, we stop by the Seagull Hotel. Two friendly travelers are drinking tea in the lobby. Mrs. Brown is from the UK, and Mr. Green is from the US. They are on a trip, too! Mrs. Brown says, I'm on holiday with my sister. In the US, people take the elevator. In the UK, we go on holiday in the summer. In the US, people go on vacation. We often eat biscuits with our tea, and in the US, people often eat cookies with milk. We use different words, but we can always understand each other with a smile." What do people in the US often eat with milk? Cookies. How can we always understand each other? With a smile. Birimo says, In Birimo Land, people say up box! Mr. Green laughs so hard that he almost drops his cup. Now, phonics. Today, let's compare four words. Lift and elevator. In the UK, people say lift. In the US, people say elevator. Elevator has a strong first part. EL, e, va, tor. Holiday and vacation. In the UK, people say holiday. In the US, people say vacation. Vacation has a strong middle part. Va, CA, tion. Next time, we will go to the City Museum and buy tickets for everyone! | objective | story timeline: L1014 (museum, 'today') and L1015 (Clock Tower, boat, lunch and museum all on one day, told to Skye tonight) put the hotel meeting before the museum; 'this evening' contradicts that; afternoon tea fits |

| 1014 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, In the UK, people say lift. Today, we visit the City Museum. It's a big stone building near the castle hill, and there's a dinosaur skeleton in the front window! First, we go to the ticket office. Ms. Emma looks at the price list and counts. We are one adult and four children: Haru, Riko, Kiko, and Birimo. She says to the staff member, One adult and four children, please. Is there a family discount? Say it with me. One adult and four children, please. Is there a family discount? Great! A discount makes the price lower. Let's read the price list. "City Museum Tickets. Adults, twelve dollars. Children aged six to twelve, six dollars. Children under six, free. Family ticket, for up to two adults and four children visiting together, thirty dollars. Open every day from nine thirty to five. Don't miss the Colour Room and the Science Centre on the second floor! The Little Theatre shows a puppet play at two o'clock." How much is a family ticket? Thirty dollars. What time is the puppet play? At two o'clock. Birimo whispers, Do creatures need tickets, too? The staff member smiles and says, Friends of all kinds are welcome, and you count as a child! So the family ticket is perfect for us, and it's cheaper, too. Now, phonics. Today, let's look at spelling. Colour. In the UK, colour has a u. In the US, it's c, o, l, o, r. Centre. In the UK, centre ends with r, e. In the US, center ends with e, r. Theatre. It's the same! In the UK, it ends with r, e. In the US, it ends with e, r. Favourite. In the UK, favourite has a u, too. | 1015 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, One adult and four children, please. Is there a family discount? Tonight, Skye flies down to the hotel garden. She wants to hear all about our day in Brightwater. Ms. Emma says, Let's tell the story in order. First, then, after that, and finally. Haru starts. We visited the Clock Tower. Then, we took a boat on the river. Say it with me. We visited the Clock Tower. Then, we took a boat on the river. Great! Visited and took tell Skye that it happened in the past. Skye wrote about her day in her notebook, too. Let's read Skye's notebook. "My Day in Brightwater. This morning, I flew over the Clock Tower and said hello to the seagulls. Then, I rested on top of the old castle. After that, I watched my friends on a river boat. They waved at me, and I waved my wing back! In the afternoon, I followed them to the City Museum. Finally, I watched a beautiful sunset over the harbor. Every trip is a story!" Where did Skye rest? On top of the old castle. Where did she watch the sunset? Over the harbor. Then Skye opens the Wing Stamp Book and says, You told your story so well! Here is your fifth stamp. Stamp! It shows a little clock tower. Only one page is empty now. Now, phonics. Let's review. Lift. In the UK, people say lift. In the US, people say elevator. Colour. In the UK, colour has a u. Memorize. Memory, drop the y, and add i, z, e. Necessary. One c and two s's. Flight 760. Read it two numbers at a time. Flight seven sixty. Mrs. Brown is flying home on Flight 760 tomorrow, so we memorize the number and wish her a good trip. Next time, we will write an email to the hotel front desk to ask about the airport bus. | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, One adult and four children, please. Is there a family discount? | 1016 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, We visited the Clock Tower. Then, we took a boat on the river. Our trip is almost over. On our last day, we need to get to Brightwater Airport. Is there an airport bus? Ms. Emma says, Let's write an email to the front desk together. I'll send it from the hotel computer in the lobby. An email to a hotel starts with Dear, and then we say why we are writing. I'm writing to ask about the airport bus. Say it with me. I'm writing to ask about the airport bus. Great! Let's read our email. "Dear Front Desk, I'm writing to ask about the airport bus. Our group of five is leaving on Saturday morning, and our flight leaves at ten thirty. Could you tell me what time the bus leaves the hotel? Is it necessary to book seats? We also have five suitcases. Thank you for a lovely holiday at the Seagull Hotel. We look forward to hearing from you. Best wishes, Ms. Emma and friends, Room 507" What day is our group leaving? Saturday morning. How many suitcases do we have? Five. Kiko asks, Should we write our home address, too? Ms. Emma says, No, we don't need to. Our room number is enough. An hour later, Mr. Lee replies. The airport bus leaves from the front door at seven fifteen, and seats are free for hotel guests. Birimo says, Mr. Lee writes emails with a smile, too! Now, phonics. Let's review. Seat 32F. Read it seat thirty-two F. Supervise. Super means over, and vis means see. When you supervise, you watch people and make sure they are safe. Address. Two d's and two s's. A, d, d, r, e, s, s. Finalize. Final plus ize. We finalize our plans for the last day. Holiday. In the UK, people say holiday. In the US, people say vacation. Next time, we will read a travel brochure and choose a tour for our last afternoon! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, We visited the Clock Tower. Then, we took a boat on the river. Our trip is almost over. On our last day, we need to get to Brightwater Airport. Is there an airport bus? Ms. Emma says, Let's write an email to the front desk together. I'll send it from the hotel computer in the lobby. An email to a hotel starts with Dear, and then we say why we are writing. I'm writing to ask about the airport bus. Say it with me. Our group of five is leaving on Saturday morning, and our flight leaves at ten thirty. Could you tell me what time the bus leaves the hotel? Is it necessary to book seats? We also have five suitcases. Thank you for a wonderful stay at the Seagull Hotel. We look forward to hearing from you. Best wishes, Ms. Emma and friends, Room 507" What day is our group leaving? A, d, d, r, e, s, s. Finalize. Final plus ize. We finalize our plans for the last day. Holiday. In the UK, people say holiday. In the US, people say vacation. Let's read our email. "Dear Front Desk, I'm writing to ask about the airport bus. Our group of five is leaving on Saturday morning, and our flight leaves at ten thirty. Could you tell me what time the bus leaves the hotel? Is it necessary to book seats? We also have five suitcases. Thank you for a wonderful stay at the Seagull Hotel. We look forward to hearing from you. Best wishes, Ms. Emma and friends, Room 507" What day is our group leaving? Saturday morning. How many suitcases do we have? Five. Kiko asks, Should we write our home address, too? Ms. Emma says, No, we don't need to. Our room number is enough. An hour later, Mr. Lee replies. The airport bus leaves from the front door at seven fifteen, and seats are free for hotel guests. Birimo says, Mr. Lee writes emails with a smile, too! Now, phonics. Let's review. Seat 32F. Read it seat thirty-two F. Supervise. Super means over, and vis means see. When you supervise, you watch people and make sure they are safe. Address. Two d's and two s's. A, d, d, r, e, s, s. Finalize. Final plus ize. We finalize our plans for the last day. Holiday. In the UK, people say holiday. In the US, people say vacation. | 1017 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, I'm writing to ask about the airport bus. Today is our last full day in Brightwater, and we have one free afternoon. Mr. Lee gives us a bright brochure from the front desk. It shows three tours for visitors. Ms. Emma says, We can only choose one, so let's read carefully. Read the brochure. Which tour would you choose? Say it with me. Read the brochure. Which tour would you choose? Great! Let's read the brochure together. "Brightwater Afternoon Tours. River Boat Tour. Ride a boat under five old bridges and see the city from the water. One hour. Castle Walk. Walk up the hill with a tour guide and enjoy the best viewpoint in town. About two hours. Wear good shoes! Market Food Tour. Taste fresh fruit, warm bread, and fish soup at the River Market food stalls. Ninety minutes. All tours start at one o'clock in front of the Old Theatre. Children must join with an adult." Which tour is the longest? The Castle Walk. Where do all the tours start? In front of the Old Theatre. Kiko says, I'd choose the River Boat Tour because I love boats. Haru wants the Castle Walk because he loves castles. Birimo wants the Market Food Tour, of course! We already took a boat yesterday, so we vote. The Castle Walk wins, and Ms. Emma promises Birimo a snack at the top. Now, phonics. Let's review. Gate 102. Read it gate one oh two. Zero is oh. Visitor. Visit means go and see, and or means a person. Excellent. Two l's, and it ends with e, n, t. Modernize. Modern plus ize. The Old Theatre was modernized last year, so it looks new inside. Theatre. In the UK, theatre ends with r, e. In the US, people write theater. Next time, we will write our own travel journals about this trip! | Hello, everyone! | 1018 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Read the brochure. Which tour would you choose? Tonight is our last night in Brightwater. Ms. Emma gives each of us a small notebook. She says, A travel journal helps you remember your trip forever. Write what you did, and then write your favourite part. Riko writes first. On the first day, we visited the market. The best part was the boat ride. Say it with me. On the first day, we visited the market. The best part was the boat ride. Great! Kiko glues her museum ticket on her page, and Haru draws the Clock Tower. Let's read Riko's journal. "My Travel Journal. On the first day, we visited the River Market. I tried a sweet orange from a food stall. On the second day, we went to the City Museum. I was surprised by the giant dinosaur. The best part was the boat ride on the river, because Skye flew right next to our boat. Tomorrow, the airport bus leaves at seven fifteen in the morning. Now I realize that polite words really open doors!" What surprised Riko at the museum? The giant dinosaur. Why was the boat ride the best part? Because Skye flew next to the boat. Birimo writes only one line. The best part was all the food. Everyone laughs. Now, phonics. Let's review. Seven fifteen. Fifteen has a strong end. Fifteen, not fifty. Visit. Vis means see. When you visit, you go and see a place or a person. Tomorrow. One m and two r's. T, o, m, o, r, r, o, w. Realize. Real plus ize. It means to suddenly understand something. Favourite. In the UK, favourite has a u. In the US, people write favorite. Next time, we will go back to the airport and use all our polite requests one more time! | Hello, everyone! | 1019 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, On the first day, we visited the market. The best part was the boat ride. This morning, we take the airport bus at seven fifteen and say goodbye to Mr. Lee. He waves from the front door with a big smile. Now we are back at Brightwater Airport. It's very busy, and we can't see the check-in counters. Ms. Emma keeps us close and stops a friendly staff member. She says, Excuse me. Would you mind helping us? Do you know where the check-in counter is? Say it with me. Would you mind helping us? Do you know where the check-in counter is? Great! That's two polite requests in a row. The staff member says, Not at all. It's next to the gift shop. Let's read the departures board. "Brightwater Airport Departures. Flight 318 to Skyport, ten thirty, gate 6, on time. Flight 452 to Seashell Bay, ten fifty, gate 9, delayed. Flight 117 to Coral Island, eleven twenty, gate 3, on time. Please check in at least two hours before your flight. Have your passport and boarding pass ready. Thank you for visiting Brightwater. Please come back soon!" What time does Flight 318 leave? At ten thirty. Which flight is delayed? Flight 452. At the counter, Kiko asks, Could I have a window seat, please? The clerk says, Certainly. Then we buy souvenirs at the gift shop. Haru chooses a Clock Tower keychain, and Riko buys a postcard for Skye. Now, phonics. Let's review. Flight 318. Read it flight three eighteen. Visa. Vis means see. A visa is a paper that lets you enter a country. Luggage. Two g's, and it ends with a, g, e. Apologize. Apology, drop the y, and add i, z, e. Elevator. In the US, people say elevator. In the UK, people say lift. Next time, we will fly home to the Airport of Wings for our big Travel Talk Show! | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, On the first day, we visited the market. The best part was the boat ride. This morning, we take the airport bus at seven fifteen and say goodbye to Mr. Lee. He waves from the front door with a big smile. Do you know where the check-in counter is? Say it with me. Would you mind helping us? Do you know where the check-in counter is? Great! That's two polite requests in a row. Flight 452 to Seashell Bay, ten fifty, gate 9, delayed. Flight 117 to Coral Island, eleven twenty, gate 3, on time. Please check in at least two hours before your flight. Have your passport and boarding pass ready. Thank you for visiting Brightwater. Please come back soon!" What time does Flight 318 leave? At ten thirty. Which flight is delayed? Flight 452. At the counter, Kiko asks, Could I have a window seat, please? The clerk says, Certainly. Then we buy souvenirs at the gift shop. A visa is a paper that lets you enter a country. Luggage. Two g's, and it ends with a, g, e. Apologize. Apology, drop the y, and add i, z, e. Elevator. In the US, people say elevator. In the UK, people say lift. Next time, we will fly home to the Airport of Wings for our big Travel Talk Show! | A+C (block rule; group reports L981-990, L991-1000, L1001-1010) | Ms. Emma reads her own teacherScript; her own narration said "Ms. Emma says / takes us / her pouch". -> I / my / me, same as say/subtitles and as L991-1000 (group). Other characters' cards, Skye's line and the email signature keep the name |

| 1020 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last time, we said, Would you mind helping us? Do you know where the check-in counter is? We're back! Our plane landed at the Airport of Wings, and today we're on the observation deck. Visibility is perfect, so we can see the white wing roof and the blue sea. It's time for the World 34 Travel Talk Show! Haru holds the microphone and tells the story of Flight 205. Riko talks about the City Museum, and Kiko shows her travel journal. Birimo shows everyone his Clock Tower keychain. Then Ms. Emma says, We learned so many polite words on this trip. Traveling is the best way to learn! Say it with me. Traveling is the best way to learn! Wonderful! Suddenly, Skye lands on the railing with a letter in her beak. Let's read the letter. "Dear friends of the Airport of Wings, Congratulations on finishing your trip! You asked politely, solved problems calmly, and thanked everyone you met. Now, children from many places are going to meet at the Hall of Wisdom. They will share ideas and talk together about our world. We would love you to join us. Please come with your teacher. Your new friends are waiting!" Where will the children meet? At the Hall of Wisdom. Who should come with the friends? Their teacher. Skye smiles and says, Congratulations! Here is your sixth stamp! Stamp! Then something amazing happens. The six stamps make a picture of a big building with a round roof. That's the Hall of Wisdom! Now, phonics. Let's review. Flight 205. Read it flight two oh five. Visibility. Visible means you can see it, so visibility means how far you can see. Recommend. One c and two m's. Memorize. Memory, drop the y, and add i, z, e. Vacation. In the US, people say vacation. In the UK, people say holiday. Goodbye, Skye! Thank you, Airport of Wings! See you at the Hall of Wisdom! | Hello, everyone!
